# EFMI Vulkan - Technical decisions

Design decisions D1 to D11 of the product itself are in `docs/plan.md`, section 3. This file records the decisions about how the project is run, and later changes to the design with their reasons.



## D-278: Queue cold character work and hold pending replacements without waiting (2026-11-15)

- Verified issues.md against the current implementation, corrected overbroad queue/deployment claims and incomplete ABI remedies in its implementation-audit section. Freeze expansion of unobserved Vulkan extension matrices; preserve the game's observed classic-pass, ordinary-descriptor and two-graphics-queue priority. I-01 remains open: completion tracking alone does not solve introduced cross-queue resource accesses.
- I-05 / BUG-142 baseline d278-before.out on 3.0.5 passes 17/20. Cold merged/texture draws incur three pipeline waits and two texture waits; own mesh incurs one pipeline wait. Correct pixels and clean core/sync validation isolate the readiness issue.
- Original CommandList.cpp 6407 substantiates custom resources once, 6553 loads files through DirectXTK, and HackerContext BeforeDraw/AfterDraw owns draw ordering. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan retains completed upload owners/publication and frame readiness explicitly; it need not hold engine resource/generation locks waiting for workers.
- Source 3.0.6 / ABI f1f9f5cf861f3caa queues every cold character variant without await and requests published texture bindings without waiting. Pending replacement draws are held until a complete frame; beside-original lists retain their caller, and explicit failed-resource fallbacks remain. The shared record entry applies this policy to merged, own-mesh and texture-only paths. Native unmatched file acquisition, game-shader twin waits outside res_mx, synchronous shader preparation and broader transaction consolidation remain separate work; this is not a claim that all recording costs vanished.
- d278-pair.out builds all four artifacts and passes tracker 764/764. d278-after.out is 20/20. A final gate placement review rebuilds the two affected binaries in d278-final-pair.out with four matching stamps. d278-regression.out draw-readiness passes cold readiness 20, own shadows 48, own forward captures 63, merged opacity 48, beside-original outputs 131, texture-only outputs 45, tracker 764, Vulkan validation 76 and text 677. No real-game, deployment, launcher, system or remote action.
- Next: move native scope dispatch behind the installed CharacterModule, separate stable recording protocol from private planners, and replace shared PipelineVariant representation with a Vulkan handle plus opaque retained owner. Test a module whose private PipelineVariant layout actually differs, not only an identical rebuilt DLL. Then continue the cross-queue proof and shared matched/unmatched draw transaction work.


## D-277: Retained graphics uniforms use application fallback across passes (2026-11-15)

- Finished the already pending D-277 change while auditing issues.md; source 3.0.5 / ABI f1f9f5cf861f3caa. Retained application graphics uniforms now preserve their normalized application fallback, recheck attachment storage at later passes, suspend conflicting requests and restore them at a later independent pass without another native list. The same retained descriptor request mechanism serves images and buffers; private file publications remain separate.
- Original CommandList.cpp 9692 retains resource bindings and 10000+ uses 16-byte constant-buffer units. DX11 provides reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan must retain the original descriptor and fold dynamic offsets exactly once when cloning it.
- Baselines on 3.0.4: d277-cpu-before.out 762/764 and d277-gpu-before.out 13/15. Real GPU descriptors are deliberately shader-unused: no undefined alias reads; CPU driver stubs verify actual handles/ranges, GPU counters verify suspension and restoration with real allocations. The initial new CPU fixture incorrectly combined a dynamic descriptor with a push layout and an older mock lacked the newly needed end barrier. Corrected to ordinary allocated dynamic descriptor sets and complete mock dispatch. The failed d277-pair.out / d277-cpu-final.out executions are not validation evidence.
- d277-cpu-final2.out passes 764/764; d277-final-pair.out builds host/inject/backend and checks four matching version/ABI stamps. d277-regression.out passes the native-uniform-persistence area, including 52 persistence checks, existing application images, graphics uniforms, tracker, core/synchronization validation and English text. Only sandbox module exchange; no game load, deployment, launcher, system or remote changes.
- Priority now follows the verified Endfield architecture review. Freeze expansion of unobserved Vulkan feature matrices. Audit actual cross-queue resource dependencies separately from per-queue completion; fix blocking draw readiness and the shared execution/ABI boundary. Game validation remains a separately authorized gate, not an offline result.


## D-276: Native input bindings require attachment storage independence (2026-11-15)

- D-275 committed and main fast-forwarded as 339c3e7. BUG-140: d276-cpu-before.out on 3.0.3 passes 743/744, accepting a sampled-only image whose distinct handle hides overlapping current attachment storage. d276-gpu-before.out passes 13/14: an input-only constant reference to the output allocation is accepted, with unchanged screen/owned pixels 6418/902. The extra descriptor is unused by the shader, so this is a valid conservative-policy baseline with normal exits and clean core/sync validation.
- Original CommandList.cpp 9692 retains the current SRV, 10051 binds it, and 10000+ sets constant-buffer ranges in 16-byte units. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. A Vulkan input-only assignment needs its own attachment-storage proof even if no output command appears in the plan. Source 3.0.4 / ABI be521eec46555682 shares D-275's ordinary allocation-range arithmetic between output plans, new graphics buffers and application image binding/refresh. Active colour, depth/stencil and enabled resolve views participate; classic and resolved imageless snapshots are retained facts. Only tracked swapchain images qualify for implicit WSI storage; unknown views/ranges refuse. Private published file ownership remains separate.
- d276-pair.out fully builds runtime/tracker/layer/backend and passes 757 checks. Its stricter secondary check regresses the existing exact-image-reference case: d276-secondary-before.out is 3/4 with correct pixels, normal exit and clean validation but a refused native list. A secondary may retain its exact original application view identity and readable descriptor state without introducing a different image access; other views still require parent attachment proof. The follow-up also prevents an unsupported active context from borrowing older framebuffer facts. d276-final-pair.out rebuilds the affected image/descriptor/output sources and tracker, relinks and passes 760/760 and four matching 3.0.4/be52 stamps. The shared utility header conservatively invalidates the existing original-runtime header cache; that dependency rebuild ran. Historical C4018 warnings are unchanged.
- d276-regression.out native-input-memory passes input allocation cases 40, tracker 760, existing application images, graphics uniforms, Vulkan validation 76 and text 673. Eight new GPU scenes cover overlap/disjoint ranges, dynamic/classic passes, pushed descriptors and completed sandbox module exchange. Existing actual application-texel sampling, reload, secondaries and module exchange also pass. Normal exits and clean validation; no game, deployment, launcher, system or remote action occurred.
- Next: retained application uniform bindings can cross into a later render pass without another native list. Current native_uniforms supplies ordering but does not re-evaluate attachment storage as persistent images do. Reproduce that boundary, retain the application's original descriptor fallback, suspend unsafe requests and restore them in a later independent pass. Broader application-image/output lists still require live layout-state preservation. Imported/external allocation identity, allocator subrange reuse after logical resource destruction, capture dimension changes and broader pass/resolve/queue adapters remain separate audits.


## D-275: Output independence requires allocation ranges, not resource types (2026-11-15)

- D-274 committed and main fast-forwarded as 9f2a522. BUG-139: d275-cpu-before.out on 3.0.2 passes 720/721, accepting an output image and a different uniform buffer bound over the same allocation bytes. An initial fixture compile error was corrected before that baseline ran. d275-gpu-before.out uses the retained 3.0.2 production DLLs and a newly built sandbox: 13/15, overlapping and disjoint bindings both redirect screen/owned geometry from 6418/902 to 0/6418. Production hashes are in build/d275-initial-dist/hashes.json; its sandbox was subsequently rebuilt. The extra descriptor is deliberately unused by the shader, so the old baseline is valid and core/sync clean; it tests conservative bound-descriptor rejection without issuing an undefined simultaneous access.
- Original CommandList.cpp 10094/10112 retains other outputs when replacing one, and 10051 binds PS SRVs in context order. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan resource types/handles alone do not prove independence: requirements from the driver, bound memory offsets and bufferImageGranularity determine ordinary allocation overlap. Reference: https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/resources.html#resources-memory-aliasing . This corrects D-271/D-272's earlier assumption that a buffer cannot alias an image.
- Source 3.0.3 / ABI be521eec46555682 queries ordinary image/buffer memory requirements, carries allocation spans in retained NativeResource snapshots and preserves original output storage metadata. Whole-list output preflight checks live/pushed descriptors, texel views, future bindings, vertex/index/indirect inputs, active attachments, original outputs and restoration snapshots. Unknown same-allocation spans and overflow cannot prove independence; linear/nonlinear ranges use granularity pages. Independent suballocations remain accepted. Published private file resources keep their separate ownership proof.
- d275-pair.out fully builds runtime/tracker/layer/backend, passing the first 739 checks and four matching stamps. Review then added preserved-output and null-only event negatives plus retained-span assertions: d275-preserved-before.out passes 741/743, proving two further omissions. d275-final-pair.out recompiles the changed target implementation and tracker against the unchanged matching objects, relinks and passes 743/743 and all four stamps. Historical character_package.cpp C4018 warnings remain unchanged.
- d275-regression.out native-output-memory passes real allocation cases 44, target resources 30, tracker 743, Vulkan validation 76 and text 671. Eight scenes cover overlap/disjoint ranges, dynamic/classic rendering, pushed descriptors and completed sandbox module exchange; every process exits normally with clean core/sync validation. Targeted compatibility checks also pass buffer SRVs (core/black/null), published constants, exact-black file/null images and depth destination changes. No game, deployment, launcher, system or remote action occurred.
- Next: apply backing-storage proof to ordinary native input changes, which can occur without an output event. Audit current image attachment-identity checks and constant-buffer inputs against real retained attachment storage before allowing broader application-image/output combinations. Imported/external allocation identity, persistent image layout preservation, capture dimension changes and broader pass/resolve/queue adapters still need separate proof. The staged build/d275-before.py application-image baseline has not run.


## D-274: Empty SRVs introduce no output image alias (2026-11-15)

- D-273 committed and main fast-forwarded as 9049bb3. BUG-138: d274-valid-before.out on 3.0.1/f910 passes 9/21. Null image bindings work alone but before/after/post combinations with output switches remain refused, preserving screen/owned geometry 6418/902. Every valid-baseline process exits normally with clean core/sync validation. The earlier d274-before.out 2/21 encountered D-201's already-known pinned VVL bug: cc_descriptor.cpp 2060 checks any immutable sampler for VUID 09506 without testing YCbCr conversion, although the sandbox sampler has no conversion. It is not a clean planner baseline. The corrected fixture uses mutable samplers without disabling validation or modifying the SDK.
- Original CommandList.cpp 9692/9720 returns a null resource for an absent SRV, and 10051 passes the null view to PSSetShaderResources; 10094 changes outputs in context order. DX11 supplies null-SRV zero reads, reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan now admits an absent SRV into the combined plan because it introduces no image alias. Whole-list descriptor preflight still proves actual kind and the enabled nullDescriptor feature before any commands. Source reference: https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkWriteDescriptorSet.html.
- Source 3.0.2 / ABI f9104b7b1d2fa5de: d274-pair.out fully builds runtime/tracker/layer/backend and the changed sandbox, passing tracker 720; four matching version/ABI stamps pass. Compilation completed during an intentional chat interruption and was checked on resumption. d274-regression.out native-output-null-images passes null combinations 71, existing native images 46, tracker 720, validation 76 and text 670.
- Thirteen new GPU scenes cover before/after/post order, core/KHR rendering, injection, classic RenderPass/RenderPass2, pushed descriptors, wrong kind, application-image refusal and completed sandbox module exchange. Exact RGB-zero pixels prove null sampling in the redirected destination. The sandbox can explicitly request robustness2 with all features false; the no-feature scene proves the device log says null descriptors off, preserves both original pixel results and reports the feature refusal. All normal exits and clean core/sync validation; immutable-sampler null validation remains limited by pinned VVL. No game, deployment, launcher, system or remote action occurred.
- Next priority is backing-memory overlap proof before expanding application-image/output combinations. Inspection found that output conflict checks compare image handles, while ImageInfo/BufInfo already record memory handles and offsets but no image allocation span. Vulkan permits distinct resources to alias a VkDeviceMemory range, including buffer/image granularity padding: https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/resources.html#resources-memory-aliasing. Reproduce with independent CPU/GPU fixtures, retain the original DX11 runtime distinction, and add explicit allocation-range checks without rejecting disjoint suballocations. build/d275-before.py stages the separate application-image combination baseline but has not run. Capture dimension changes and broader pass/resolve/queue adapters remain offline work.


## D-273: Permit independently owned read-only images in output lists (2026-11-15)

- D-272 committed and main fast-forwarded as bef42fa. BUG-137: d273-before.out on 3.0.0/f910 passes 11/22 with the corrected exact-black oracle. Published file images bind alone, but combining them with before/after/post output changes triggers the blanket image-mixture refusal. Screen/owned geometry stays 6418/902 instead of redirecting; the black texture never controls the full destination. All normal exits and clean core/sync validation.
- Original CommandList.cpp 11995 creates a compatible SRV for the retained resource, 10051 binds it and 10094 preserves other outputs while changing one. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan admits only retained, published read-only sampled file images whose allocations have no storage or attachment usage. These cannot introduce application attachment aliases. Whole-list descriptor preflight, shader shape/type checks, upload publication waits and exact current-descriptor conflict checks remain in place. Application-image references and mutable images retain their refusal.
- Source 3.0.1 / ABI f9104b7b1d2fa5de: d273-pair.out fully builds runtime/tracker/layer/backend and passes tracker 720; four matching version/ABI stamps pass. d273-regression.out passes existing native images 46, tracker 720, validation 76 and text 669. Combined file images report 73/74: the mutable-image negative already preserves the original but reports an earlier compatible-usage refusal, not the expected mixture refusal. Correcting that specific reason assertion gives 4/4 in d273-mutable.out on unchanged production binaries. Passing unaffected cases are not repeated.
- Sixteen new GPU scenes cover command order, core/KHR rendering, injection, classic RenderPass/RenderPass2, pushed descriptors, exact black texture data, wrong array shape and descriptor kind, application/mutable image refusals, file reload and completed sandbox module exchange. Reload changes the DDS from white to black and verifies the new generation and exact black pixels in the redirected destination. Normal exits and clean core/sync validation. The module exchange is sandbox-only; no game, deployment, launcher, system or remote action occurred.
- Continue null image SRVs beside output changes. An absent image introduces no alias, while existing descriptor preflight must still enforce actual kind and the device's nullDescriptor feature. build/d274-before.py prepares a baseline. Application-image alias state, capture dimension changes and broader pass/resolve/queue adapters remain offline work.


## D-272: Use actual descriptor layout types to prove buffer destinations (2026-11-15)

- D-271 committed and main fast-forwarded as 838ecab. BUG-136: d272-before.out on 2.9.9/f910 reports 11/22. A published buffer SRV works alone but combining it with output changes rejects the plan with the blanket non-constant descriptor reason; original screen/owned pixels remain 6418/902. Before, after and post combinations all fail. Its black-pixel assertion was subsequently corrected: band_pixels counts differences from the nonblack clear color, not nonblack pixels. d272-baseline-audit.out rechecks the retained complete bytes of all five baseline scenes, clean validation and actual refusal reasons; the old black case has zero exact black pixels. Processes exit normally.
- Original CommandList.cpp 2032 recognizes Buffer/BufferEx SRV ranges, 9692/9720 retains the current SRV and resource, and 10051/10094 sets PS resources and outputs in context order. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan now classifies SRV destinations using the retained bound-set layout: buffer slots cannot introduce image attachment aliases, including when null. The later whole-list descriptor preflight still proves actual pipeline compatibility, kind, resource retention, range, stride, usage and access ordering before commands are emitted.
- Source 3.0.0 / ABI f9104b7b1d2fa5de follows the required single-digit counter after 2.9.9. d272-pair.out fully builds runtime/tracker/layer/backend and passes tracker 720; four matching version/ABI stamps pass. d272-regression.out passes constant combinations 64, tracker 720, validation 76 and text 668, but buffer combinations report 69/73. d272-pixels.out proves black/null data already creates 6418 exact black pixels: that failure was the band-count oracle. The fixed-struct sandbox shader has no runtime array stride, so stride 8 is not a valid stride-mismatch negative; stride 3 instead proves rejection of an incomplete file element. d272-corrected.out passes all 12 checks for the three affected scenes on unchanged production binaries. Passing unaffected scenes are not repeated.
- Sixteen buffer scenes cover before/after/post output order, core/KHR rendering, injection, classic RenderPass/RenderPass2, pushed descriptors, white/black published data, actual null buffers and a completed sandbox module exchange. Wrong kind, incomplete file stride, application writable-buffer hazards and mixed image assignments preserve original pixels with precise refusal reasons. All normal exits and clean core/sync validation. These are sandbox results; no game, deployment, launcher, system or remote action occurred.
- Continue published read-only file images beside output changes. Their independently owned images cannot be application attachments, and their existing publication/descriptor path already retains ownership and waits. build/d273-before.py prepares the baseline. Application-image aliases, persistent image transitions, capture dimension changes and broader pass/resolve/queue adapters remain offline work.


## D-271: Preflight constants independently of output images (2026-11-15)

- D-270 committed and main fast-forwarded as 56e43ce. BUG-135: the corrected d271-valid-before.out on retained, hash-verified 2.9.7/f910 passes 7/13, refusing combined output/constant-buffer lists. Its native_targets.cpp is identical to 2.9.8. The initial d271-before.out and d271-regression.out mixed fixture used a VS alias under a PS override, so their failures are not valid target-planner evidence. The corrected fixture uses a PS alias. d271-partial.out on the first 2.9.9 build also passes 7/13, exposing the second loop that incorrectly treats constants as output slots.
- Original CommandList.cpp 10000+ sets constant-buffer ranges in 16-byte units; 10094/10112 sets one output while retaining the others. These operations coexist in one context. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan permits typed constant assignments beside outputs, excludes them from output-event preparation and retains the existing whole-list descriptor preflight for layout kind, range, usage, ownership and graphics dependencies. Image descriptor mixtures remain refused before commands are emitted.
- Source 2.9.9 / ABI f9104b7b1d2fa5de: d271-pair.out fully builds runtime/tracker/layer/backend. d271-final-pair.out recompiles native_targets.cpp against the unchanged matching objects, relinks and passes tracker 720; four matching version/ABI stamps pass. The first area run already passes unchanged ordinary outputs 52, graphics uniforms 16, tracker 720 and validation 76. After the loop fix, d271-final-regression.out passes mixed bindings 64, validation 76 and text 667. Passing unaffected steps are not repeated.
- Thirteen mixed cases cover original order, before/after output assignments, core/KHR rendering, injection, classic RenderPass/RenderPass2, published uniform bytes, wrong descriptor kind, image refusal and a completed sandbox backend exchange. The file shader uses a color component impossible for the original uniform payload, independently proving that the buffer controls pixels in the switched destination. All normal exits and clean core/sync validation. The swap is sandbox-only; no game, deployment, launcher, system or remote action occurred.
- Continue mixed outputs with retained buffer SRVs: an SRV backed by a proven buffer cannot alias a framebuffer image, but the current blanket non-constant refusal still rejects it. build/d272-before.py prepares a fresh baseline. Unknown/null/image SRVs and writable-resource hazards retain their guards. Capture dimension changes and broader pass/resolve/persistent-image/queue adapters remain offline work.


## D-270: Represent a null scene-depth read in the composite shader (2026-11-15)

- D-269 committed and main fast-forwarded as a486511. BUG-134: d270-before.out on 2.9.7/f910 passes 23/27 with actual device null descriptors off. A warm null-depth scene stops at 58 captures/composites instead of 98 and reports a feature requirement; restoring depth only reaches 68. Every process exits normally with clean core/sync errors and complete target bytes. The replay explicitly requests robustness2 with all features false; the layer respects that application request.
- Original CommandList.cpp 10051 binds the current PS resource, including null. EFMITTL Capture retains the current scene references, and shaders/composite.hlsl 35 loads SceneDepth. DX11 supplies null-SRV zero reads, reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan now compiles a separately keyed depthless composite using the existing typed-zero SPIR-V rewrite, binds a retained valid spare depth view and transitions that view even when all four layers exist. No null descriptor is needed for this scene-depth slot; incomplete cold-layer history is unchanged. This is not a claim that every backend feature works without robustness2.
- Source 2.9.8 / ABI f9104b7b1d2fa5de: d270-pair.out fully builds runtime/tracker/layer/backend; tracker 720 and all four version/ABI stamps pass. d270-regression.out capture-no-null passes ordinary depth history 27, actual feature-disabled history 32, capture snapshots 31, tracker 720, validation 76 and text 666. Warm and warm-restored scenes now complete 98 captures/composites; cold and cold-then-restored scenes retain zero as the original incomplete-layer rule requires. All normal exits and clean core/sync errors; known interface warnings remain visible.
- d270-parity.out independently compares every final target of all five history scenes with nullDescriptor on and off: all bytes match. New fixture flags affect only the test child's Vulkan device request. No game, module exchange, deployment, launcher, system or remote action occurred.
- Continue combined output/constant-buffer native lists. prepare_native_targets currently refuses every descriptor assignment mixed with outputs, even a typed constant-buffer binding that cannot alias an image. Read the original OM/constant-buffer setters, reproduce against 2.9.8, then permit only the independently validated buffer case while retaining image-alias guards. Capture dimension changes, broader pass/resolve/persistent-image and queue adapters remain open; they are offline engineering work.


## D-269: Share deferred captures across mesh routes and preserve every replay diagnostic (2026-11-15)

- D-268 committed and main fast-forwarded as 6db3757. BUG-132: d269-before.out on 2.9.6/f910 passes 10/13: ordinary meshes execute but no deferred captures/composites do. Source 2.9.7/f910 shares retained capture preparation with merged meshes, keeps the ordinary application's vertex/bone inputs, prepares four private capture variants, snapshots each accepted output marker and validates immediate output work before publishing deferred captures.
- Original CommandList.cpp 1642 draws with the current context; EFMITTL Capture 983/984 retains SceneColor/SceneDepth and saves/restores outputs after the layer is complete, without requiring a Core component. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan owns the retained geometry, image views, descriptor snapshots and recording-order output transaction explicitly; there are no character-specific runtime tables.
- d269-pair.out fully builds runtime/tracker/layer/backend; tracker 720 and four 2.9.7/f910 stamps pass. d269-focused.out reaches 12/13, with 29 captures/composites but identical original-mesh pixels. That fixture has no independent visibility proof. The final owned box retains actual game shaders and valid ordinary bone inputs: low/high opacity changes forward color0 only, mask channels/inversion have exact equivalent bytes, and every target readback is complete.
- d269-regression.out passes own shadows 48, merged deferred outputs 54, tracker 720, matched Mod outputs 158, validation 76 and text 665; its own-capture step crashes. A fresh retry also crashes. BUG-133: the second symbolized stack directly names pinned VVL logging.cpp 130, where duplicate_message_count_map is read/written before debug_output_mutex. The first stack names VVL allocation/free operations; the same cause is possible but not proven. Matching the existing Sandbox child setting, replay_capture now sets VK_LAYER_DUPLICATE_MESSAGE_LIMIT=0, preserving all diagnostics and bypassing that racy map without disabling validation. The independent no-EFMI/no-device messenger probe completes 100 rounds with both limits and does not reproduce the crash. Initial DLL hashes and both symbolized traces are retained under build/d269-*; no system setting or external SDK binary changed.
- d269-unlimited.out completes all nine scenes with clean core/sync errors but passes 62/63: the mixed fixture incorrectly expects an immediate forward draw to contribute depth. The corrected fixture draws the immediate box with depth unbound and restores depth before capture. d269-mixed.out passes 14/14; d269-final-capture.out passes 63/63 for all nine scenes on the unchanged 2.9.7 pair. Mixed immediate/deferred execution changes scene colour while keeping depth intact. The failed reports remain distinct; passing unchanged area steps are not repeated. Known interface warnings remain visible. No real-game or module-exchange acceptance is claimed.
- Continue removing the compositor's nullDescriptor dependency for a retained complete layer whose current scene depth is null. Use original null-SRV zero semantics, retained valid descriptor storage and a separately keyed zero-read composite shader; cold incomplete history must remain unchanged. Changing capture dimensions and broader pass/resolve/persistent-image/queue adapters remain open. No deployment, game, launcher, system or remote action.


## D-268: Share library shadow execution and explicitly preprocess generated Vulkan specialization (2026-11-15)

With both connections, d268-final-focused.out reaches 12/13 and 58 shadow draws, but still produces opaque pixels at both opacities.

The original CommandListShadow uses current IniParams and restores its mask; ShaderRegex.cpp 366 links shader groups independently of components, and HackerContext.cpp 492 executes them. CommandList.cpp 2573 compiles original source with stage macros. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan shares mask ownership/preparation between mesh routes, preserves ordinary vertex/bone inputs and ordered output markers, and opts generated native shaders into separate Vulkan preprocessing. Ordinary Mod preprocessing and the original DXBC reference stay unchanged; native preprocessing has a distinct cache key.

Seven cases per mesh route cover low/high opacity, output restoration, zero/one mask channels and inversion. Equivalent cases preserve every target byte, a zero mask removes visible depth, and all target readbacks are complete. The ordinary route uses an owned box with valid original vertex/bone inputs and actual game shaders; neither route requires runtime character tables. Normal exits and clean core/sync errors; known interface warnings remain separate. No game or new module-exchange acceptance is claimed for these new opacity cases.

Changing capture dimensions and broader pass/resolve/persistent-image/queue adapters remain open. No deployment, game, launcher, system or remote action.


## D-267: Retain output state at each deferred capture marker (2026-11-15)

- D-266 committed and main fast-forwarded as 5e19e00. BUG-129: fresh d267-before.out on 2.9.4/f910 passes 10/13. Nulling then restoring depth before a deferred capture still rejects 28 plans, drops items from 3108 to 2744 and loses all 28 captures/composites. Normal exits, clean core/sync errors and complete fenced targets; the original baseline folders remain distinct from d267-focused-*.
- Original CommandList.cpp 10094/10112 preserves other output bindings; DrawCommand 1642 uses that point's context. EFMITTL Capture 983/984 retains scene references only after the layer is complete. DX11 supplies reference-counted lifetime with deferred destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. The merged executor now snapshots outputs at each accepted capture marker, retains immediate and deferred positions separately, and validates the immediate draw transaction before publishing captures. Cold incomplete-layer history and private descriptor restoration remain intact.
- Source 2.9.5 / ABI f9104b7b1d2fa5de: d267-pair.out builds tracker/layer/backend, tracker 720/720 and four matching stamps pass. ## D-246: Retained DSV bindings preserve exact roles, lifetime and layouts (2026-11-14)

- Original SetResource binds one DSV while retaining all RTVs. DX11 provides retained/deferred lifetime, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan keeps independent DSV original/active view state beside colors, retains event resources through completion, explicitly transitions introduced DSVs and restores them on null/original replacement and host End. An explicit initialization bit handles zero-color scopes correctly; all shared layout changes require a matching host/module ABI pair.
- Depth/stencil image format determines transition aspects because attachment roles ignore the view aspectMask. A combined destination needs a common proven layout for both aspects, which transition and restore together. Exact per-role pipeline formats, identity, single sample, 2D one-subresource coverage, no active attachment/descriptor overlaps and no alternate saved/original view aliases remain mandatory. Null originally ignored resolve fields are normalized as in BUG-110.
- A nonnull assignment creates missing pointer roles only from defined pipeline formats, reindexing the local complete view context before later ordered events. Adding a role is a real event even if the actual view handle is unchanged. No format means no inferred role. Entire plans validate before any GPU command. Existing legacy single-queue and query/mapping/pass/persistent-image limits remain.
- Separate full fenced original/source D32 or stencil readbacks prove real destinations and pre/post order. An empty initialization pass plus explicit dependency provides defined bytes when original pointer roles are absent. Module-exchange coverage waits for the requested generation and normal process completion; a disk build alone is not a loaded module.

- Original IfCommand reevaluates conditions separately in pre and post. GPU fixtures use a pre-set per-draw source flag so a source-save global changed during pre cannot accidentally enable a main-target post restore. The corrected test changes no production behavior; real unproven-layout refusals remain intact.

## D-244: DSV null changes both actual aspects; filter timing follows the original (2026-11-14)

Refreshing inside the output event would violate the original timing.

## D-211: Narrow DDS data uses aligned staging regions (2026-11-14)

- Original CommandList LoadFromFile/DirectXTK FillInitData passes each mip/layer's row and slice pitches to DX11; deferred ownership, hazard tracking and context order come from that runtime. Vulkan keeps its retained upload/publication chain and explicit copy layout.
- Decode R8_UNORM, R16_UNORM and RGBA16_UNORM DDS (DXGI61/56/11), matching already represented WIC formats. Float sampled consumers remain required; integer/depth/writable consumers are not broadened.
- Transfer-only queues require four-byte buffer offsets (vkCmdCopyBufferToImage VUID-commandBuffer-07737). An internal region plan preserves original DDS stride, skipped-mip gaps and each source offset, adds alignment padding and moves bytes backwards in place. Existing RGBA/BC payloads need no padding. Worker allocations and arena slices charge the padded span; fence/timeline readiness and retained lifetime remain.
- Tests read every narrow texel independently from the GPU with sixteen-byte readback packing, including both upload paths, legacy/native shapes and demotion. Native shaders sample every cell, F10 and alternate descriptor/queue paths. Exact builds and final evidence are in CURRENT_STATE.

## D-210: Retained application views cover their full mip/layer range (2026-11-14)

- Original ResourceCopyTarget GetResource/SetResource keeps and rebinds the exact stage SRV, including array/cube shape. DX11 supplies deferred reference lifetime, hazard tracking and one-context order. Extend D-206's explicit Vulkan recording-local ownership/layout path rather than infer global state from recording order.
- Accept exact non-depth float sampled 2D, 2D-array, cube and cube-array consumer shapes. Normalize remaining-range constants and bound the nonzero mip*layer product to1024. Every view cell participates in layout validation, temporary sampling transitions, restored layouts and persistent descriptor refresh; partial views use their original base mip/layer.
- CUBE_COMPATIBLE is the only added safe creation flag. Unknown extensions, mutable flags, unsupported usages/scalars, queue/synchronization2 graphs, unknown local layouts and unresolved attachment overlaps remain guarded. The capture adapter keeps its separate explicit image.flags/subresource checks and is not broadened by this view feature.
- Fixture reads distinct values from every view cell before/after source writes, GENERAL refresh and application rebind, including partial views and shape mismatch refusal. Baseline12/24; exact builds and final evidence are in CURRENT_STATE. No game/deployment action.

## D-209: Native file resources preserve sampled array/cube shapes (2026-11-14)

- Original CommandList.cpp LoadFromFile (6594) and FillOutTex2DDesc (11727 onward) load full DDS resources and preserve their SRV shape. DX11 supplies reference-counted deferred lifetime, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan keeps the existing owned upload, timeline publication and retained descriptor chain.
- Native file declarations select full layers through an explicit cache-key mode. Legacy character material slots keep D-149's first-layer 2D view and cannot alias the native view of the same file. Both staging paths copy every selected mip of every layer using the original DDS layer stride; skipping top mips must not collapse the gaps between layers.
- Create cube-compatible images and exact 2D/2D-array/cube/cube-array views. Require device layer limits and the application's enabled imageCubeArray feature; no feature is silently enabled. Read-only float sampled consumers must match dimensionality/array state and typed format. Writable/mutable/depth-comparison views remain outside this adapter.
- BUG-100: standalone immutable samplers are initialized by descriptor allocation, so descriptor cloning must skip their ordinary updates. Combined sampler image updates and explicit push initialization remain. Original CustomShader Run saves/restores sampler state through DX11 setters; that is not permission to overwrite a Vulkan immutable descriptor. Keep retained layout/sampler owners.
- Standalone all-layer compressed readback/cache/demotion/feature checks pass394/394; native baseline6/12, first implementation3/12 exposed BUG-100. Exact corrected builds and final scope are maintained in CURRENT_STATE. No game/deployment action.

## D-208: Capture GPU indirect arguments without inventing draw counts (2026-11-14)

- Original FrameAnalysisContext logs indirect argument resource/offset and DumpBuffer copies full allocations. DX11 owns deferred resource lifetime, hazard tracking and immediate-context order. Vulkan uses the existing retained host staging and completed submission publication.
- Export full argument and optional count allocations once per resource/kind at each graphics pass end; compute captures follow each indirect dispatch. Geometry uses name their GPU argument snapshots, byte offsets, command size, stride and maximum draw count, instead of treating recording-time max counts as actual GPU draw values. Secondary recording identities remain intact.
- Overflow-safe argument/count ranges, transfer usage, resource owners, pipeline recipes, static vertex input and all D-202 queue/completion constraints remain required. End-of-pass bytes do not reconstruct earlier argument values modified in the pass. No source usage flags or application queue features are changed.
- Baseline7/11, failed cases39/39 after. Final capture-area checks all pass, including121 indirect checks and clean core/synchronization validation; CURRENT_STATE lists exact builds/artifacts. No real-game or deployment claim.

## D-207: Exchanging code preserves the host installation root (2026-11-14)

- Original IniHandler resolves d3dx.ini and relative override_directory from migoto_handle (4458,
  4681-4690). Reloading shaders/configuration does not relocate that installation. DX11 supplies
  reference-counted deferred GPU lifetime, hazards and context order separately from path resolution.
- The exchanged module obtains the host directory from HostApi::log's code address during entry,
  without a new ABI field or reliance on a fixed DLL name. Its module_dir implementation returns
  that root. Backend Mods/Core/settings discovery and relative paths therefore keep the host's
  configuration base when the code DLL is copied elsewhere.
- ShaderFixes resolves relative/default settings through the same base, independently of the owned
  toolchain directory used for validation. It must not interpret a relative settings path against
  the sandbox/game process working directory. No launcher/system setting or external file changes.
- Baseline relative Mods/settings exchange 3/7 (d207-path-before.out), including an active module
  that lost its sources/ShaderFixes. Final failed case7/7; module-paths checks application images76, native files42, tracker260 and text573 all pass. Pair/tracker are2.3.7, ABI97d3399f4a32ac23 unchanged; see CURRENT_STATE for exact artifacts.

## D-206: Application image references follow current layouts, with persistent refresh (2026-11-14)

- Read original ResourceCopyTarget::GetResource/SetResource in DirectX11/CommandList.cpp. DX11 supplies deferred reference-counted GPU lifetime, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan must retain image/view/memory and mutable sampler owners, know the image subresources' current layout, and explicitly order newly introduced reads.
- Each native descriptor element preserves the desired image and its retained original application value. Refresh before every draw/dispatch, including unmatched shaders; clone/rebind changed descriptors without mutating sets referenced by recorded commands. Suspend only the unsafe element, keep its desired request, and allow a later safe layout to resume it. A new application bind supersedes the request. Allocation failure suppresses the affected call rather than issue it against stale descriptors.
- The application's statically accessed original descriptor may establish a recording-local valid-application layout precondition, never overwrite contradictory facts. Follow the original element through native clones; the replacement's requested layout is not evidence. Current GENERAL/SRO bind exactly. Temporary transitions normalize overlapping views to unique image/mip/layer cells, use retained command owners, leave application facts unchanged and restore at compute/pass end. Mixed GENERAL layouts are not converted to satisfy a wider view.
- First scope is one application graphics queue without synchronization2, owned optimal float32 non-depth 2D sampled views and supported usage/creation state. New graphics transitions must be prepared outside the pass; attachment overlap or unresolved attachment scope refuses. Unknown fresh-recording layouts require future submission-graph work. Existing file-image uploads remain on their publication path.
- Shared image scope and reflection-cache metadata participate in the host/module ABI. Source/reflection caches are bounded, barrier tracking remains opt-in, and no game load or deployment is part of this work. Baselines and exact verification are in CURRENT_STATE.

- Module exchange exposed BUG-098: use the existing owned GPU tools directory for ShaderFixes validation, rather than the exchanged DLL directory. Observe new-module shader binds before the test screenshot; an expired test notice must not be mistaken for bad image pixels. Final evidence includes 62 non-swap application image checks and 7 exchange checks, with separate build/regression scopes recorded in CURRENT_STATE.

## D-205: Image layout facts belong to a command recording (2026-11-13)

- Original ResourceCopyTarget::GetResource/SetResource obtains and retains views while DX11 supplies deferred reference-counted GPU lifetime, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan cannot infer an image's current layout from the last descriptor update or the order CPU threads record command buffers.
- Track explicit image barriers/event waits and implicit classic-pass transitions per resource identity, aspect, mip and array layer inside each recording. ExecuteCommands composes secondary effects at the execution point; resetting either recording cannot mutate a prior composition. Unknown ownership transfers/extensions or bounded-state exhaustion refuse knowledge rather than fabricate a shader-read layout. Activate this additional state only when native application-image use asks for it.
- Synchronization2 equal-layout barriers with equal queue-family fields ignore their layout values, including legacy VkImageMemoryBarrier calls on a synchronization2-enabled device (https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkImageMemoryBarrier.html). Event source halves do not publish a completed transition. A later known local transition can reestablish facts.
- Classic implicit transitions affect framebuffer layers, or the union of used multiview layer bits (https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/renderpass.html). A pass attachment outside the current subpass may be before/after an automatic transition, so it is unknown until a current use or pass end. Never transition unused attachments merely because their description has a finalLayout. Dynamic rendering does not implicitly transition layouts.
- This is the foundation for D-206, not a cross-queue synchronization graph or native application-image binding acceptance. Initial standalone baseline 10/21; implementation range/barrier checks 28/28. Host callback/pass cases bring the tracker to 252/252; live INI 221/221, native resources 89/89 and Vulkan validation 76/76 pass with the 2.3.5 pair. See CURRENT_STATE for exact artifacts.

## D-204: Output attachment capture is independent of shader reflection (2026-11-13)

- Original FrameAnalysisContext::DumpRenderTargets obtains OM output views/resources directly. A shader is not a prerequisite for their identity. DX11 supplies reference-counted deferred GPU lifetime, hazard tracking and one-context order; Vulkan obtains owned views and final layouts from the retained classic-pass description and uses D-202 staging/barriers/completion ownership.
- A primary can have no graphics pipeline during a clear-only pass or after executing secondary graphics commands. Missing/failed shader reflection suppresses unresolved descriptor image captures, but never prevents independently known stored output attachments from being considered. Store-discard targets are not exported, missing TRANSFER_SRC still refuses, and no scope/queue/layout guard is relaxed.
- Dedicated two-clear GPU baseline 9/15 confirms both missing-pipeline paths with a passing ordinary-graphics control. Final 2.3.4 target checks pass 22/22 with synchronization validation; existing GPU resources 55/55, geometry 65/65, tracker 206 and standard validation 76 also pass. See CURRENT_STATE for the exact builds.

## D-203: Complete GPU IA allocations with separate draw-use metadata (2026-11-13)

- Read original FrameAnalysisContext::DumpVBs, DumpIB and DumpBuffer. It stages complete resources and retains input-layout attributes, buffer strides/offsets, topology and draw ranges. DX11 supplies reference-counted deferred GPU destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan reuses D-202 retained staging, explicit barriers and completed timeline publication.
- At classic pass end, capture each original application's used vertex/index buffer once per kind and attach all direct draw uses, retaining secondary command identities. Keep complete bytes; interpret index regions through bind_offset + first * index_size and count * index_size. Indexed vertex-use first/count fields describe the index range. Do not label this per-draw snapshots or replacement mesh output.
- Static pipeline attributes decide which VB slots are used. Incomplete pipeline recipes, dynamic input/topology, indirect argument ranges and untracked VB slots are explicit refusals. Resource usage and creation flags are never changed by capture. Copy sizes need not be divisible by four (https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkBufferCopy.html); the odd-sized allocation fixture checks its final byte.

## D-202: Host-owned completed staging exports for newly recorded analysis work (2026-11-13)

- Original FrameAnalysisContext::DumpBufferImmediateCtx maps completed staging, then writes GPU data. Deferred contexts retain their resources with COM ownership. DX11 supplies deferred GPU destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order; Vulkan must explicitly retain source allocations and staging, put copies after the relevant work, restore image layouts, and wait for completion before reading.
- The host owns the capture engine and callback, not the exchangeable backend. One frame's exclusive analysis folder receives raw buffers/images and a resource manifest. Each accepted operation has its own staging allocation; an appended host timeline signal covers the actual submission. A background worker polls completion and writes only completed bytes. Command owners and secondary dependencies retain jobs; shutdown idles the device before clearing recordings and draining the worker.
- Initial adapter accepts one-time non-simultaneous recordings on one unprotected application graphics queue. Require Synchronization2 disabled: stage-limited app signals could expose a resource before the added transfer copy, and widening them may create a new wait cycle. Do not pretend a record-time layout query solves that graph. Arbitrary multiple queues, swapchain ownership, external/extension resources, replay, depth/array/multimip subresources and exceptional descriptor flags are refused. Captures are post-application-call or end-of-pass, not snapshots after every internal custom draw/dispatch. Existing recordings are not retroactively instrumented.
- Byte/job budgets bound capture allocations; no resource usage bits or system state are silently changed. Test-only fixtures opt into transfer-source usage. Refusals are data in resources.jsonl, never substituted CPU snapshots. Final D-202 checks: GPU analysis 55/55 with synchronization validation, tracker 206, character 115/377, validation 76 and historical hunting analysis 12/12. Core/KHR timeline lookup is required for the Vulkan 1.1 fixture; failing closed before that fix produced no exports.

## D-201: Native sampled file images use published owned 2D views (2026-11-13)

- Original CustomResource::LoadFromFile selects DirectXTK DDS/WIC, loader colour space and bind flags; CreateCompatibleView checks view/resource types. DX11 supplies reference-counted deferred GPU destruction, hazard tracking and one-context order. Vulkan reuses bounded asynchronous residency and explicit completed-publication semaphore waits, retaining view/image/allocation and publication owners for recorded commands.
- Accept typed non-array 2D sampled images whose consumer expects float32 color data. Preserve actual uploaded mip dimensions and shader-read layout. Refuse arrays/cubes, writable images, incompatible explicit view formats and non-sampled declarations. Arbitrary application images still require subresource layout tracking. This is shared behavior without character tables.
- Verification is rendered pixel and compute data, including F10, pushed and secondary commands. Source-register metadata now also records image numeric/depth type without changing DX11 register inference. This decision is source work until CURRENT_STATE lists completed checks.

- Depth=2 means unknown, not a depth image (SPIR-V OpTypeImage, https://registry.khronos.org/SPIR-V/specs/unified1/SPIRV.html). Permit 0/2 native shader compatibility and color uploads only with no depth-reference instructions, including sparse operations. Native-only twins may retain the exact application's push layout; no private set is appended. BUG-096 records the regressions and final verification.

## D-001: Changes stay inside the project; EFMI, the game and EFMI MCP are read-only

- Date: 2026-10-11.
- Decision: writes are limited to `F:\efmi vulkan` and to `F:\vulkan` (Vulkan headers / SDK). The EFMI/XXMI installation, the launcher configuration, existing mods, the game installation and `F:\EFMI MCP` are only read.
- Reason: same boundary the user set for the sibling project; this project needs real data from those places but no change to them.

## D-002: Memory split by seven responsibilities

- Date: 2026-10-11.
- Decision: overview, architecture, current state, decisions, issues, tasks and session log are seven Markdown files in `.ai-memory/`; the root `AGENTS.md` is the entry point for resuming and maintaining them, and `CLAUDE.md` imports it.
- Reason: the user asked for a memory system modelled on `F:\EFMI MCP\.ai-memory`. Only the structure was taken from there; no content of that project was copied.

## D-003: English only, no Chinese characters in the workspace

- Date: 2026-10-11 (user).
- Decision: file names and contents are English; `scripts/check_text.py` fails the regression suite when it finds CJK characters. The plan written in Chinese earlier the same day was translated to `docs/plan.md` and the Chinese file removed.
- Note: the conversation with the user stays in Chinese.

## D-004: Commit locally after every verified change; no remote

- Date: 2026-10-11 (user, stated twice).
- Decision: each change that passed its verification is committed unprompted. No remote is configured and nothing is pushed until the user names a repository. `docs/plan.md` is tracked (the user chose this explicitly; it differs from the EFMI MCP project, where the plan stays local).

## D-005: Vulkan headers live in `F:\vulkan`, outside the repository

- Date: 2026-10-11 (user: install to `F:\vulkan`).
- Decision: `scripts/fetch_vulkan_headers.ps1` makes a shallow clone of `KhronosGroup/Vulkan-Headers` at tag `vulkan-sdk-1.4.321.0` into `F:\vulkan\Vulkan-Headers`. Build scripts look for headers in this order: `%VULKAN_SDK%\Include`, `F:\vulkan\Include`, `F:\vulkan\Vulkan-Headers\include`.
- Not done: the LunarG SDK installer was not run. It needs elevation, accepts licence terms and changes system environment variables and the registry, which is the user's to do. If the user installs the SDK into `F:\vulkan`, the build picks it up without a change.
- Consequence: there is no `glslc` and no validation layer on this machine. The sandbox builds its two shaders with a small SPIR-V builder in its own source.

## D-006: A live probe for development, modelled on EFMI MCP

- Date: 2026-10-11 (user asked for a tool like the DX11 one to help development).
- Decision: the read-only diagnostics of the layer are served over a named pipe `\\.\pipe\efmi-vk-<PID>` with the same framing as the EFMI MCP probe (`[u32 header_len][u32 payload_len][header JSON][payload]`). `server/efmi_vk` holds the client, a CLI and a stdio MCP server; `.mcp.json` registers the server for this workspace.
- Reason: gates G2 and G3 are questions about live data (threads, submits, index bytes, bindings). Answering them through tools an agent can call is faster and more repeatable than reading log files, and the sandbox gives the same tools a target that needs no game.
- Boundary: the probe is read-only. It does not change what the application draws.

## D-007: The sandbox is the regression target; the real game is a separate layer of evidence

- Date: 2026-10-11.
- Decision: every probe feature gets a sandbox test with known expected values (the sandbox writes `expected.json`). Results from the sandbox are never reported as results from the game.
- Reason: the game cannot be used for routine testing (anti-cheat, account risk, user's decision per run).

## D-008: One Python build script instead of batch files

- Date: 2026-10-11.
- Decision: `scripts/build.py` finds `vcvars64.bat` (through `VCVARS` or `vswhere`) and the Vulkan headers, runs `cl` for the layer and the sandbox and writes the layer manifest. The regression suite calls it.
- Reason: one place for the header search order of D-005; the tests can build into another directory with `--out` while a process still holds the DLL in `dist/`.

## D-009: Two modes of the layer; draw records are always kept in probe mode

- Date: 2026-10-11.
- Decision: `EFMI_VK_MODE=passthrough` hooks only device destruction and present and starts no pipe; it is the mode for gate G1. In probe mode every command buffer keeps the records of its draws all the time, not only while a capture is armed.
- Reason: a capture then never depends on when a command buffer was begun, which matters for engines that record ahead of the frame boundary. The original record cost was about 200 bytes and one vector push per draw; 0.4.0 adds descriptor allocation ids and shared layout references, so that old estimate no longer describes the current record. Heavy-scene overhead remains unmeasured (T-012).

## D-010: Shadows are fed at submit time and only for index buffers by default

- Date: 2026-10-11.
- Decision: buffer writes recorded into a command buffer are applied to the shadow when that command buffer is submitted, reading the staging source at that moment. Only buffers with index usage get a shadow unless `EFMI_VK_SHADOW_USAGE` widens the mask.
- Reason: what the staging buffer holds at submit time is what the GPU copies; a shadow for every buffer of a game would cost too much memory. The DX11 dump shows large shared index buffers bound at byte offsets (plan fact 14), so the shadow is per buffer with an interval set of known bytes, and hashes are taken over sub-ranges.
- Open: whether the game's uploads are visible to the CPU at all is gate G3; LIMIT-007 lists what is not covered.

## D-011: The game gets the DLL by injection with export redirection; the layer entry is the fallback

- Date: 2026-10-11 (the user asked whether loading can work by injection, as with d3d11).
- Decision: a second entry was added to the same binary. Loaded with `LoadLibrary`, the DLL redirects the export table entries of `vulkan-1.dll` in memory and calls the original addresses from its hooks. `efmi_vk_inject.dll` selects this entry by its file name, `efmi_vk_layer.dll` the layer entry. For the game the plan is the launcher's extra-library injection, which already brings the user's DX11 probe into `Endfield.exe`.
- Reason: no registry entry and no system-wide change; independent of elevation (LIMIT-012); the same launch flow the user already runs; the redirection technique is the one the EFMI MCP probe uses on `d3d11.dll` in the real game.
- Cost: the DLL has to be in the process before the engine resolves its Vulkan functions, which is not known for the game (LIMIT-013). A device created earlier is served by the passive device and not tracked.
- Settings: `efmi_vk.cfg` next to the DLL, because the launcher cannot hand environment variables to the game; an environment variable still wins, which the tests rely on.

## D-012: What the project needs is installed into `F:\vulkan` without asking

- Date: 2026-10-11 (user).
- Decision: dependencies such as headers, SDKs and tools go into `F:\vulkan` directly. This replaces asking before each download.
- Boundary kept: nothing an installation would write outside that folder (registry, system environment variables, system directories). If an installer cannot be confined to the folder, that is reported instead of run.

## D-013: Descriptor snapshots are explicitly at submit; bytecode follows pipeline lifetime

- Date: 2026-10-11.
- Decision: the first T-011 increment tracks ordinary descriptor writes/copies and snapshots contents when a captured command buffer is submitted. Dynamic offsets are taken at bind time and split by layout. Shader bytes have shared ownership between their live module and pipelines; ids do not rely on handles remaining unique.
- Reason: descriptor sets are references, not inline copies of their contents. Querying them later from the pipe could silently return another frame's data; retaining immutable capture JSON avoids that. Module destruction after pipeline creation is normal and must not remove shader diagnostics prematurely.
- Limits: submit-time visibility does not establish G3's record-time premise. In 0.4.0, templates, pushes and descriptor buffers discarded stale tracked knowledge; D-015 subsequently adds template/push decoding. Descriptor buffers remain unsupported. A small declared-binding SPIR-V reader is not full reflection. Details: `docs/probe-resources.md`.
- Historical deployment choice, superseded by D-014: because run 3 ended before 30 minutes, this implementation round built/tested 0.4.0 under `build/next/` and kept `dist/` at 0.2.0 passthrough.

## D-014: Reuse run 3 as initial loading evidence; defer the long-duration observation

- Date: 2026-10-11 (user).
- Decision: the user confirmed exiting run 3 before 30 minutes after scene changes, then instructed the project to use the previous run's data. Accept it as initial G1 loading evidence and continue development and short probe preparation without requiring another 30-minute passthrough session. The original duration was a project observation target, not a technical requirement.
- Evidence boundary: the historical run used 0.2.0 passthrough. Loading and device creation were logged; scene changes and the early exit were reported by the user. Exact duration, total frames, long-duration stability, probe-mode game behavior and real-character capture data remain unverified. Do not relabel historical results as a new test.
- Deployment: after confirming no game process remained, preserved the old DLLs/settings/log under `build/backups/`, rebuilt and regression-tested 0.4.0 in `dist/`, and set `dist/efmi_vk.cfg` to probe. A sandbox using that actual configuration captured a frame successfully.
- Boundary kept: this decision prepares the next session; it does not authorize the agent to start or inject the game. Each real-game launch still follows the user's on-the-spot instruction. Launcher settings remain read-only, and blocked loading still stops the project.

## D-015: Push contents are retained at recording time; resource visibility stays at submit

- Date: 2026-10-11 (implementation of the next T-011 increment).
- Decision: template definitions own their copied entries and retained layout metadata. Ordinary template updates use the same decoder as writes. Each push update creates immutable contents retained by subsequent draw records, separate for each command buffer and bind point. Partial writes preserve compatible elements; layout disturbance and reset invalidate them. Immutable sampler-only push bindings start undefined.
- Reason: looking up a push bank's final state at submit would give earlier draws the last update's descriptors. Recording immutable values prevents this and survives secondary-buffer flattening. A push template's bind point comes from its creation metadata, not from the currently bound graphics pipeline.
- Diagnostic boundary: `snapshot` is `record` for pushes and `submit` for ordinary sets; `resource_snapshot` is always `submit`. Retaining a buffer handle/range is not evidence that its bytes were readable at record time. This does not satisfy G3 b/c by itself.
- Scope: standard buffer/image/texel/sampler descriptors; unknown templates and unsupported layouts discard stale state. Descriptor buffers and specialized types remain unimplemented. GPU and CPU coverage are recorded separately in `docs/probe-resources.md`.

## D-016: Retain XXMI hosting with explicit adapter boundaries; correct sample scope

- Date: 2026-10-11, M0 static dependency assessment.
- Decision: keep plan D4's hosted XXMI frontend for M3. The pinned v1.2.2 parser/expression sources share D3D-shaped state, globals and resource code; they are not an isolated portable library. WWMI-DX12 shows a concrete host arrangement but uses an older baseline and a substantial adapter. Define state/query/resource/input boundaries and test new Pool/Region/ElementFormat semantics in a later compiled prototype. A reduced interpreter stays a fallback if that prototype fails.
- Evidence: local d3d11.dll exactly matches the official v1.2.2 archive and source tag a7847eae82aaac32175c9a7d199673d5c9323bcd. Reference host commit 113f42cc2dc5702dbba624ee16abac96d68ed3b5. No extraction, compilation or runtime equivalence of a host is claimed. Details and all input hashes: docs/compatibility-inventory.md and its JSON companion.
- Scope correction: the enabled mod folder contains arcane.ini and k/1.ini, an HLSL image panel. The main file configures 13 slots but defines component ids 0-11. Full-folder acceptance includes the panel; do not silently disable it or count character-only rendering as complete compatibility. Existing mod files remain read-only.
- Update, 2026-10-12: the bounded native host feasibility prototype compiles, links and passes 23 original-implementation CPU checks. Keep the hosted approach; no reduced interpreter is needed for this checkpoint. It is standalone, with reference headers as scaffolding and no layer/graphics/mod integration. Exact tested semantics and remaining adapter work: docs/xxmi-host-prototype.md.

## D-017: Whatever the probe keeps is bounded, and there is a tracking level below full

- Date: 2026-10-11, review of 0.4.0 / 0.5.0 before the first probe session in the game.
- Decision: SPIR-V is kept only up to `shader_budget` (default 512 MiB); a capture keeps descriptor contents only up to the request's `descriptor_budget` (default 64 MiB); destruction and pool reset do work proportional to what is destroyed, not to what exists; `resources = 0` is a tracking level between full probe and passthrough.
- Reason: the sandbox has two shader modules, a handful of descriptor sets and five draws. The game has a 206 MB pipeline cache, and its counts are unknown until the first session. Unbounded retention and full-table scans under the exclusive resource lock were the parts most likely to hurt the game, and a session that goes badly should be able to fall back without losing the draw data.
- Not decided: the defaults are guesses to be revised with the first real numbers. A budget reached in the game is reported (`shaders_not_retained`, `descriptors_omitted`), not hidden.
- Kept as it was: the per-update cost of descriptor tracking (exclusive lock and map updates per written element). Its size in the game is unknown; `efvk_api_usage` shows the update rate.

## D-018: What the first game data settles in the plan

- Date: 2026-10-12, after the first probe session (CURRENT_STATE).
- D5, threading: the engine records on one thread in the observed scene and `Player.log` says `jobified=0`. The mod lock stays in the design, but bring-up does not need a single-threaded mode forced from outside. To be re-checked in the open world.
- D6, hashes: index data is read from the application's own mapping; the shadow path was not needed for any draw. Shadows stay for the case of device-local index buffers, which this scene does not have.
- D7, texture slots: mapping by resource name is ruled out, the SPIR-V carries no names. The mapping has to come from binding order and a per-shader profile, checked against the DX11 baseline.
- D3, two-phase execution: its premise looks right. Instance config and bones are in mapped memory and all per-frame data arrives without buffer copies. It is not proven until T-017 shows the bytes at submit and at record time.
- D8, vertex input: confirmed as needed; the game's blend stream has stride 12 with 8-bit indices, the mod's has stride 16 with 16-bit indices.
- Compute: 47 dispatches per frame, all outside render passes and none needed by the character's vertex shader for bones. The merged skeleton does not depend on them.

## D-019: What the DX11 baseline settles in the plan

- Date: 2026-10-12, after pairing a DX11 frame of the sample mod with the Vulkan frame (`docs/dx11-baseline.md`, facts 15 to 19).
- D7, texture slots: a mod's `ps-tN` is translated through a table from (Vulkan fragment shader, DX11 slot) to (set, binding), per pass and including bindings of the global set, because the mod assigns its slots in every pass and under DX11 they land on whatever that pass has there (fact 20). Replacing by role ("the diffuse map") is rejected: it would not show what DX11 shows. The table is data produced by `scripts/dx11_baseline.py` from paired frames, not something the layer derives at run time: binding order is not register order and there are no names. Where two textures look alike the entry stays open until content identity exists (T-018). A draw whose fragment shader has no table entry is left unreplaced and reported.
- Instance window: taken from set 2 binding 0 at the draw's dynamic offset, 256 bytes; found by its place in the layout. The DX11 core's per-shader search for the slot has no counterpart.
- Bone buffer: identified as a buffer (the one storage buffer of 8,413,184 bytes the character pipelines bind in set 0), not by a binding number.
- D3, CPU work: the two compute steps of the core are a copy of bone rows per component and frame and a patched copy of 16 constants per matched draw. Both are done on the CPU before the draw is replaced, provided T-017 shows the bytes are there in time.
- D8, vertex input: the variant changes the stride of the blend binding and the format of its unsigned-integer attribute and binds 32-bit indices; attributes are identified by binding and format, not by semantic.
- Not decided: how the fragment stage keeps the game's bone buffer and instance window (T-019).

## D-020: How a capture keeps buffer contents

- Date: 2026-10-12, with layer 0.6.0 (T-017).
- Bytes are kept as they are at submit, not at record: submit is the last moment the layer sees before the GPU may read, and one read per distinct range serves all draws of the command buffer. Record time contributes a checksum per range only; that answers whether the data was complete then at four bytes per range instead of a second copy.
- The request names what to follow (set, binding, byte offset, count, stride, window size) instead of the layer knowing the game's instance entry. The game's rule lives in `scripts/collect_probe.py` as a preset. A later Core-VK will read the same places through its own code; the probe stays generic.
- The first bytes of the followed range are a separate item (`entry`): the game's range is 64 KiB with the entries of many draws, and a comparison of the whole range would call a draw's data incomplete because of its neighbours.
- Selection at record time is by region hash, computed there a second time while a snapshot capture is in its frame. Rejected: reading every draw at record time (cost in every frame of the capture for draws nobody asked about) and selecting at submit only (no record-time evidence).
- Bounded like everything else the probe keeps (D-017): budget in bytes, size limit per uniform range, clamps on every requested size; past the budget a range is still checksummed and compared.

## D-021: What the second probe session settles in the plan

- Date: 2026-10-12, after layer 0.6.0 ran in the game (facts 21 and 22).
- D3, bone import: done by the CPU from the application's own mapped memory. No GPU read-back (T-013 stays closed for bones), no compute pass. The premise of the two-phase execution is measured, not assumed.
- When to read: both moments are valid in the observed scene. The instance entry and the bones of a component may be read when its matched draw is recorded; reading at submit is always at least as good and remains the fallback if another scene shows late data. Which of the two M2 uses is an implementation choice; M2 starts with the read at record time because it needs no deferred work, and keeps the comparison of 0.6.0 as a check.
- How much to read: exactly `VertexGroupCounts[component]` bones of three rows from the entry's offset, as the DX11 core does. Never a fixed window: bytes behind a palette belong to something written later.
- What not to use: the order or distance of palettes in the bone buffer, which changes per frame.
- Scope: one scene with one standing character. The decision is revisited if a later session shows `different` inside a palette or an entry.

## D-022: The pipeline variant gives the vertex stage a set of its own

- Date: 2026-10-12 (T-019, fact 23).
- The vertex module of a replaced draw's pipeline is rewritten so that its storage buffer and its instance window are bound at bindings 0 and 1 of the first set the pipeline does not use (set 3 for the sample character). The layer binds one set of its own there: the merged skeleton as storage buffer and its patched instance entries as dynamic uniform buffer, one 256-byte slot per replaced draw with 64 KiB of room behind the last slot, because the shader's block is 4096 constants long. The fragment module is not touched and reads the game's bone buffer and window, as it does under DX11.
- The variant's pipeline layout keeps the game's set layouts and push constant ranges and adds one set layout at the end, so the sets the game has bound stay valid for it.
- The two variables are found by structure: the only storage buffer of the vertex module, and its uniform buffer at set 2 binding 0. A vertex module with no or several storage buffers, or without that uniform buffer, gets no variant; its draw is left to the game and reported.
- Rejected: replacing the shared bindings. It needs a copy of the global set (about 50 bindings) for every pass that draws the character, and 20 of the 45 draws have a fragment shader that would then read the merged skeleton at a patched offset instead of the component's own first bone.
- Cloning a set and overriding bindings is still needed for textures (set 1, and now and then a binding of the global set, fact 20). That mechanism is separate from this decision.
- Evidence is offline: four words per module, `spirv-val` on 21 real vertex shaders. Creating and drawing with such a pipeline is the first thing M2 checks.

## D-023: Disable upload hashing after the required identity capture; measure spikes separately

- Date: 2026-10-12, third Vulkan probe session, user-reported intermittent stutter during loading and steady display.
- T-018 now has its real content evidence: all four open sample slots resolve. Set image_hash = 0 in the prepared dist/ configuration for the next process; the existing process retains its original setting. Preserve full resource tracking for the first comparison.
- The measured 8.1 seconds is cumulative time inside upload processing, including reads and locks. A steady 3-second interval advances 360 presents with only 596 microseconds added there. Do not claim that average 120/s disproves stutter or that hashing caused every reported spike.
- Add bounded host present-interval diagnostics in 0.7.1 (T-020), including recent percentiles/peaks, lifetime threshold counts and capture/hash tags. This is host timing, not GPU/display timing. No performance fix is considered game-verified until a new user-started comparison session.

## D-024: Preserve buffer evidence while measuring probe-only work

- Date: 2026-10-12, after the user reports smooth movement in confirmed 0.7.1 passthrough and stutter in resource-off probe.
- Remove proven unnecessary copy work without discarding readable fallback data: test source visibility before allocating record-time storage, and copy directly into the target shadow at submit. Keep unmap snapshots and submit-time priority. The game's individual stall source remains unproven.
- Add opt-in profile_tracker measurements in 0.7.2, default off, with cumulative category counters and a fixed 64-event slow history on the present diagnostic's QPC clock. Exclude forwarded driver calls. Account for profiling overhead, nesting and descheduling; never equate the sum to frame time.
- Keep the smooth passthrough run as evidence. Any diagnostic probe candidate requires a user-started game comparison after sandbox verification. Disabling buffer snapshots is a separate capability tradeoff, not a silently applied fix.

## D-025: Accelerate detected WC reads while preserving snapshot evidence

- Date: 2026-10-12, after 0.7.2 foreground world timing correlates the 129 ms peak with three expensive unmaps and a zero-byte copy-record wait. The global resource lock makes these snapshot reads relevant to unrelated recording work.
- Keep the 16 MiB unmap limit, index shadow mask, record fallback and submit priority. Do not lower data coverage to make the performance comparison appear successful.
- Classify the complete mapped range once using VirtualQuery. Use SSE4.1 streaming loads only for committed PAGE_WRITECOMBINE mappings on capable CPUs, with aligned loads, exact prefix/tail handling and memory fences. Recompute on remap, clear on unmap; unknown/mixed/cached ranges use memmove. This changes the copy implementation, not Vulkan synchronization or lock ordering.
- Apply the helper to every mapped-read path, including unmap, record fallback, submit and direct probe reads; CPU shadows still use memmove. Expose the available strategy and current per-buffer WC classification for verification.
- Local headless Vulkan measurements validate the mechanism (type 2: 24.023 to 1.270 ms for 8 MiB), not real-game smoothness. Type-4 device-local reads remain expensive. Keep profile_tracker on for the next comparable game run and ISSUE-005 open; source profiling defaults stay off. Full evidence: docs/pacing-tracker-session.md.

## D-026: At unmap, copy staging buffers only (WITHDRAWN by D-027: the premise was wrong)

- Date: 2026-10-12, after reviewing 0.7.1 to 0.7.3 against the buffer inventory of the 0.7.2 game session.
- The copy at unmap exists for staging buffers. Its rule (transfer-source bit, at most 16 MiB) also matched the game's mesh pools, and they were where the bytes went: 2,612 generations on 22 pools against 87 on 76 staging buffers. The rule is now: transfer source, optionally transfer destination, no other usage, wholly inside the mapped range, within the limit.
- This lowers coverage on purpose, which D-024 and D-025 declined to do. The reason is different from theirs: they would not trade evidence for a better-looking measurement; here the copy of a pool was never the feature's purpose, its one use (a hash while the pool is unmapped) has not occurred in any game bundle, and no layer that has to run during normal play could afford it. What is lost is reported per draw (`hash: null`, source `none`) and counted (`counts.unmap`), not hidden.
